used intermediate width 20 for MNIST and 40 for EMNIST.

Appendix B
NTK Feature Similarity Experiments

Let
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be two sets of NTK features and define correlation as
\frac{|\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle|}{\|\mathbf{a}\|\|\mathbf{b}\|}
.
Then the average correlation between datasets
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
is
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{|\langle\mathbf{a}_{i},\mathbf{b}_{i}\rangle|}{\|\mathbf{a}_{i}\|\|\mathbf{b}_{i}\|}/n
.
Figure
8
plots the average correlation between original MNIST and permuted MNIST as a function of permutation size.
We see that the average correlation is monotonic in permutation size — extremely high for low permutation size and extremely low for high permutation size. This provides evidence of the connection between the centered permuted MNIST problem and task similarity in the NTK regime.

Figure 8:
Results of the permuted MNIST NTK feature similarity experiment.

Appendix C
Supplementary Materials for our Analytic Results (Section
2
)

Throughout the appendix, we denote the singular-value decomposition of a given
\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
by
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
for
{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
.
The number of nonzero entries on the diagonal of
{\mathbf{\Sigma}}
is
d=\operatorname{rank}\left({\mathbf{X}}\right)
.

Recall Theorem
3
.

Let
{p\geq 4},d\in\left\{1,\dots,p\right\},m\geq 2
.
Define
\mathcal{X}_{p,d}\triangleq\left\{\,\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}\mid n\geq\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf{X})=d\,\right\}
.
Define
the
D
imensionality
o
f
T
ransformed
S
ubspace

\alpha\triangleq\frac{m}{p}
as our proxy for task dissimilarity
and
\beta\triangleq 1-\frac{d}{p}
as our proxy for overparameterization.
Then, for any solution
{\mathbf{w}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}
(Assumption
1
),
the (normalized) worst-case expected forgetting per Def.
2

(obtained by Scheme
1
) is

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{X}_{p,d}\end{subarray}}\!\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})}{\|{\mathbf{X}}\|^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\|^{2}}\!=\!\alpha\bigg(2+\beta\left(\alpha^{3}+11\alpha-6\alpha^{2}-8\right)+\beta^{2}\left(-5\alpha^{3}+22\alpha^{2}-30\alpha+12\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\beta^{3}\left(5\alpha^{3}-18\alpha^{2}+20\alpha-6\right)\bigg)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{p}\right),

where
\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}
projects
\mathbf{w}^{\star}
onto the column space of
\mathbf{X}
.
Notice that
\|{\mathbf{X}}\|^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\|^{2}
is a necessary scaling factor, since the forgetting

\left\|{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}_{2}\!-\!\mathbf{y}}\right\|^{2}=\left\|{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}_{2}\!-\!\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right\|^{2}
naturally scales with
\|{\mathbf{X}}\|^{2}
and
\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\|^{2}
.
The exact expression (without the
\mathcal{O}
notation) appears
in Eq. (
9
) in Appendix
C
.

Proof outline.

We start our proof (below) by showing that the expected forgetting is sharply upper bounded as,

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left({{\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg({\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}+\left(d-1\right)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}}\bigg)\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\left({\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right)_{i}}^{2}}_{=\left\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right\|^{2}}\,,

where
{\mathbf{e}}_{1},{\mathbf{e}}_{2}
are the two first standard unit vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
This directly implies that,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})}{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right\|^{2}}\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}}_{\text{solved in Lemma~\ref {lem:at-most-three-vectors}}}+\left(d-1\right)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}}_{\text{solved in Lemma~\ref {lem:at-most-four-vectors}}}\,.

Each of these two expectations is essentially a polynomial of the entries of our random orthogonal

\mathbf{Q}_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p},\mathbf{Q}_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
which form the random operator

\mathbf{O}=\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}

as explained in Eq. (
2.1
).

We compute these two expectations
(in Lemmas
6
and
8
) by employing
exact
formulas for the integrals of monomials over the orthogonal groups in
p
and
m
dimensions

(
Gorin 2002
)
.
Our derivations often get complicated, and so we split them into three appendices:

1.

Appendix
C
(below):
Derivations of more complicated expressions involving the operator
\mathbf{O}
.

2.

Appendix
D
:
Derivations and properties of monomials of general random orthogonal matrices, mostly using results from
Gorin 2002
.

3.

Appendix
E
:
Derivations of auxiliary expressions (mostly involving
{\mathbf{Q}}_{m},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
) that are used as building blocks in multiple derivations.

Proof for Theorem
3
.

Starting from Eq. (
5
), we show that for any given
{\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star}}

it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tfrac{1}{{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})&=\tfrac{1}{{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\big\|{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right)\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\big\|^{2}\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\big\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right)\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\big\|^{2}\,,\end{split}

(7)

where, importantly, the inequality saturates when all the nonzero singular values of
\mathbf{X}
are identical.
Plugging in the SVD,

i.e.,
of
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{U}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
, we get

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\big\|{\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right)\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\big\|{{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right)\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\big\|^{2}\,,

where the last equality stems from spectral norm properties (recall that
\mathbf{V}
is an orthogonal matrix).

Following our definition of
\mathbf{O}=\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}

(Eq. (
2.1
)) and since
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
is sampled
uniformly
from the orthogonal group
O(p)
, we notice that
\mathbf{O}
and
\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{V}
are identically distributed.
Hence, we can rewrite the above expectation as

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\big\|{{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right)\underbrace{{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}_{\triangleq{\mathbf{v}}}}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\left[\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}=\left({\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\end{subarray}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{v}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\left[\begin{subarray}{c}\text{Pythagorean theorem}\end{subarray}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left({{\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{v}}}\right)^{2}\underbrace{\left\|{{\mathbf{e}}_{i}}\right\|^{2}}_{=1}\,.

(8)

Remark 4

(Ease of notation)
.

For simplicity, in the equation above and from now on, we omit the explicit subscript notation whenever it is clear from the context of our derivations what the expectation pertains to.
For instance, instead of writing

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left[F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\right]
we can simply write

\mathbb{E}\left[F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\right]
.

As another example, we often analyze (for
i\neq j
) expressions of the following spirit,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}\sim O\left(p\right),\mathbf{Q}_{m}\sim O\left(m\right)}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\sim\mathcal{S}^{p-1}\left(p\right):\mathbf{u\perp v}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{m}\sim O\left(m\right)\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{v}\right)^{2}\,,

where the last step follows from the angle-preserving property of orthogonal operators (
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
).
For the sake of simplicity, we take the liberty to also write the expectations above as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{u\perp v}}\left(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{v}\right)^{2}\,.

Finally, when the dimensions are clear from the context, we interchangeably write matrices in the two following forms:

\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{0}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{0}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\text{, and }\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right]\,.

Back to the proof.

Focusing on just one term from the above Eq. (
8
),
we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{k=1}^{d}v_{j}v_{k}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}v_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}+

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{j\neq k=1}^{d}v_{j}v_{k}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)}_{=0,\text{ by Lemma~\ref {lem:zero_fifth}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}v_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{v_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}}_{j=i}+\underbrace{\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]\setminus\left\{i\right\}}v_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}}_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=v_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]\setminus\left\{i\right\}}v_{j}^{2}\,.

We are free to use
{\mathbf{e}}_{1},{\mathbf{e}}_{2}
instead of
{\mathbf{e}}_{i},{\mathbf{e}}_{j}
(for
i,j\in\left[{d}\right]
)
due to the exchangeability of different rows of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
.
For instance, notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\mathbf{O}\right)\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\!\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}^{\top}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\!\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left(\mathbf{I}\!-\!\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)^{\!2}\!.

That is, the expression is a function of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{d}
which are the first
d
rows of the random
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and are entirely exchangeable (see Prop.
9
).

Going back to Eq. (
8
),

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left({{\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{v}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(v_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]\setminus\left\{i\right\}}v_{j}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]\setminus\left\{i\right\}}v_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\left(\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]}v_{j}^{2}\right)-v_{i}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}\left(d\sum_{j=1}^{d}v_{j}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}\left(d-1\right)\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg({\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}+\left(d-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}}\bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}\,.

Interestingly, in this worst-case scenario,
the direction of
\mathbf{w}^{\star}

that lies in the column span of
\mathbf{X}
,

i.e.,
\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}=\mathbf{V}\underbrace{{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}_{={\mathbf{v}}}

does not play a role, but only its scale

\left\|{\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right\|=\left\|{\mathbf{V}{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|=\left\|{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|=\sum_{i=1}^{d}v_{i}^{2}

(since
{\mathbf{v}}
is only nonzero in its first
d
entries).
Normalizing by this scale, we get,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})}{\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\right\|^{2}}\leq\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}}_{\text{solved in Lemma~\ref {lem:at-most-three-vectors}}}+\left(d-1\right)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{2}\right)^{2}}_{\text{solved in Lemma~\ref {lem:at-most-four-vectors}}}\,,

where we remind the reader that the inequality saturates when all the nonzero singular values of
\mathbf{X}
are identical.

By plugging in the two lemmata and after some tedious algebraic steps, we get the final bound of,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{X}\in\mathcal{X}_{p,d}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{O}}F(\mathbf{O};\mathbf{X},\mathbf{w}^{\star})}{\|{\mathbf{X}}\|^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}^{\star}}\|^{2}}\\
&=\tfrac{m^{4}p^{2}\left(2+p+p^{2}\right)-2m^{3}p\left(24+10p+13p^{2}+p^{4}\right)+m^{2}p\left(240+230p-15p^{2}+50p^{3}-2p^{4}+p^{5}\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&=\tfrac{m\left(p^{6}-28p^{5}+47p^{4}-324p^{3}-60p^{2}-240p-576\right)+2p\left(288+120p-90p^{2}+71p^{3}-6p^{4}+p^{5}\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{m^{4}p\left(-7p-2-6p^{2}\right)+m^{3}\left(16p^{4}+18p^{3}+74p^{2}+92p+48\right)+m^{2}\left(-11p^{5}-223p^{3}-267p^{2}-302p-240\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{m\left(2p^{6}-3p^{5}+168p^{4}-33p^{3}+728p^{2}+1124p+192\right)+\left(74p^{4}-240p-576-490p^{3}-252p^{2}-24p^{5}\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&\hskip 10.00002pt2d^{2}\cdot\tfrac{m^{4}p\left(6+5p\right)-2m^{3}\left(8p^{3}+13p^{2}+9p+18\right)+m^{2}\left(15p^{4}+24p^{3}+106p^{2}+162p+36\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&\hskip 10.00002pt2d^{2}\cdot\tfrac{m\left(-3p^{5}+3p^{4}-129p^{3}-191p^{2}-198p-144\right)+p\left(288+246p-15p^{2}+26p^{3}-p^{4}\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&\hskip 10.00002ptd^{3}\cdot\tfrac{m^{4}\left(-5p-6\right)+m^{3}\left(18p^{2}+34p-12\right)+m^{2}\left(-20p^{3}-46p^{2}-39p-42\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+\\
&\hskip 10.00002ptd^{3}\cdot\tfrac{m\left(6p^{4}+10p^{3}+96p^{2}+154p+60\right)+\left(2p^{4}-30p^{3}-32p^{2}-144p-144\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}\,.\end{split}

(9)

The above is the exact expression for the worst-case forgetting.
To reach the
\mathcal{O}
notation, we assume that
p\gg 1
, and so we are left with the most significant elements of each product.
That is, we show that,

\displaystyle\approx\tfrac{m^{4}p^{2}\left(p^{2}\right)-2m^{3}p\left(p^{4}\right)+m^{2}p\left(p^{5}\right)+m\left(p^{6}\right)+2p\left(p^{5}\right)}{p^{8}}\!+\!d\tfrac{m^{4}p\left(-6p^{2}\right)+m^{3}\left(16p^{4}\right)+m^{2}\left(-11p^{5}\right)+m\left(2p^{6}\right)-24p^{5}}{p^{8}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2d^{2}\tfrac{m^{4}p\left(5p\right)-2m^{3}\left(8p^{3}\right)+m^{2}\left(15p^{4}\right)+m\left(-3p^{5}\right)+p\left(-p^{4}\right)}{p^{8}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd^{3}\tfrac{m^{4}\left(-5p\right)+m^{3}\left(18p^{2}\right)+m^{2}\left(-20p^{3}\right)+m\left(6p^{4}\right)+\left(2p^{4}\right)}{p^{8}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{m^{4}-2m^{3}p+m^{2}p^{2}+mp^{2}+2p^{2}}{p^{4}}+d\tfrac{-6m^{4}+16m^{3}p-11m^{2}p^{2}+2mp^{3}-24p^{2}}{p^{5}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2d^{2}\tfrac{5m^{4}-16m^{3}p+15m^{2}p^{2}-3mp^{3}-p^{3}}{p^{6}}+d^{3}\tfrac{-5m^{4}+18m^{3}-20m^{2}p^{2}+6mp^{3}+2p^{3}}{p^{7}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{m^{4}-2m^{3}p+m^{2}p^{2}+mp^{2}+2p^{2}}{p^{4}}+\tfrac{d}{p}\tfrac{-6m^{4}+16m^{3}p-11m^{2}p^{2}+2mp^{3}-24p^{2}}{p^{4}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)^{2}\tfrac{5m^{4}-16m^{3}p+15m^{2}p^{2}-3mp^{3}-p^{3}}{p^{4}}+\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)^{3}\tfrac{-5m^{4}+18m^{3}-20m^{2}p^{2}+6mp^{3}+2p^{3}}{p^{4}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{m^{4}-2m^{3}p+\left(m^{2}+m+2\right)p^{2}}{p^{4}}+\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)\tfrac{-6m^{4}+16m^{3}p-11m^{2}p^{2}+2mp^{3}}{p^{4}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)^{2}\tfrac{5m^{4}-16m^{3}p+15m^{2}p^{2}-\left(3m+1\right)p^{3}}{p^{4}}+\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)^{3}\tfrac{-5m^{4}+18m^{3}p-20m^{2}p^{2}+\left(6m+2\right)p^{3}}{p^{4}}

\displaystyle=\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{4}\!-\!2\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{3}+\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{2}\!+\!\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)\tfrac{1}{p}\!+\!\tfrac{2}{p^{2}}\!+\!\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)\!\left(\!-6\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{4}\!+\!16\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{3}\!-\!11\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{2}\!+\!2\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)\right)\!+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)^{2}\left(5\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{4}-16\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{3}+15\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{2}-3\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)-\tfrac{1}{p}\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\left(\tfrac{d}{p}\right)^{3}\left(-5\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{4}+18\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{3}-20\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)^{2}+6\left(\tfrac{m}{p}\right)+\tfrac{2}{p}\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\alpha^{4}-2\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}+\left(1-\beta\right)\left(-6\alpha^{4}+16\alpha^{3}-11\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2\left(1-\beta\right)^{2}\left(5\alpha^{4}-16\alpha^{3}+15\alpha^{2}-3\alpha\right)+\left(1-\beta\right)^{3}\left(-5\alpha^{4}+18\alpha^{3}-20\alpha^{2}+6\alpha\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\tfrac{1}{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha\Big(2+\beta^{3}\left(5\alpha^{3}-18\alpha^{2}+20\alpha-6\right)+\beta^{2}\left(-5\alpha^{3}+22\alpha^{2}-30\alpha+12\right)+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\beta\left(\alpha^{3}-6\alpha^{2}+11\alpha-8\right)\Big)+\mathcal{O}\left(\tfrac{1}{p}\right)\,.

∎

Lemma 5
.

Let
i\in{\left[{d}\right]}
and let
j,k\in\left[{d}\right]
such that
j\neq k
.
It holds that

\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)=0\,.

Proof.

The expectation can be decomposed as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]\,,

where the last step holds because
j,k\in\left[{d}\right]
and therefore

{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}={\mathbf{e}}_{j},\,{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}={\mathbf{e}}_{k}
.

Following the definition of
\mathbf{O}
(Eq. (
2.1
)), the first expectation becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{Q}_{m}}\bigg[{\mathbf{e}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\underbrace{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}_{\triangleq\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\bigg]\,.

Since
j\neq k
, we must have either
j\notin\left\{i,k\right\}
or

k\notin\left\{i,j\right\}
(or both).
Denote the relevant rows of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
by

{{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\triangleq\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}}
,

{{\mathbf{q}}_{j}\triangleq\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}}
,

{{\mathbf{q}}_{k}\triangleq\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}}

and notice that they are independent of
\mathbf{Q}_{m}
(or
\mathbf{A}
).
Without loss of generality,

j\notin\left\{i,k\right\}
.
The above expectation becomes

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{Q}_{m}}\big[{\mathbf{q}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\big]
,
where
{\mathbf{q}}_{j}
appears only once (an odd number).
By Cor.
11
, the expectation vanishes.

Quite similarly, the second expectation becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{t}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{q}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{k}\right]\,.

Notice that both
i,t
appear an
even
number of times in (each of) the above expectation(s).
Since
j\neq k
,
at least one out of
j,k
appears an odd number of times
(either one, three, or five)
in each of the above expectations.
Again, by Cor.
11
, this expectation vanishes.
Clearly, the same holds for the third expectation.

The fourth expectation is,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell,t=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{\ell}{\mathbf{e}}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{t}{\mathbf{e}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell,t=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{q}}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{j}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{i}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{q}}_{k}\right]\,.

Notice that
i,\ell,t
appear an
even
number of times in (each of) the above expectation(s).
Since
j\neq k
,
at least one out of
j,k
appears an odd number of times
(either one, three, five, or seven)
in each of the above expectations.
Again, by Cor.
11
, this expectation vanishes.

∎

C.1
Deriving
\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}

Lemma 6
.

Let
p\geq 4,m\in\{2,\dots,p\},{d\in\left[{p}\right]}
,
and let
\mathbf{O}
be a random transformation sampled as described in Eq. (
2.1
).
Then,
\forall i\in\left[{d}\right]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{m^{4}\left(p^{2}+2p\right)-2m^{3}p^{3}+m^{2}\left(p^{4}-4p^{3}+20p^{2}-24\right)+m\left(3p^{4}-10p^{3}+63p^{2}+6p-72\right)+2\left(p+1\right)\left(p^{3}-11p^{2}+38p-24\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{d\left(p+1\right)\left(-2m^{4}+6m^{3}p-2m^{2}\left(p-1\right)\left(2p-5\right)-12m\left(p^{2}-3p+3\right)-8\left(p^{2}-8p+6\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{d^{2}\left(4mp\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

Proof.

We decompose the expectation as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}-2\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}\,,

and derive each of its three terms separately in the following subsections.

By adding these three terms and by simple algebra, we get the required result,

\displaystyle=\tfrac{m^{2}-2mp-m+p^{2}+2p+2}{p\left(p+2\right)}-2\tfrac{\left(p-m\right)\left(d\left(-m^{2}+2mp+3m-6\right)+m^{2}p-2mp^{2}-3mp+p^{3}+5p^{2}+8p-8\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{3\left(m+4\right)\left(m+6\right)+\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m+2\right)\left(m^{2}-2mp-4m+p^{2}+10p+36\right)}{p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)\left(-72+m^{4}\left(-1-2p\right)-72p-20p^{2}-20p^{3}+m^{3}\left(6+16p+8p^{2}\right)+m^{2}\left(-47-70p-34p^{2}-10p^{3}\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)\left(m\left(42+136p+114p^{2}+22p^{3}+4p^{4}\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)d\left(4mp\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{m^{4}\left(p^{2}+2p\right)-2m^{3}p^{3}+m^{2}\left(p^{4}-4p^{3}+20p^{2}-24\right)+m\left(3p^{4}-10p^{3}+63p^{2}+6p-72\right)+2\left(p+1\right)\left(p^{3}-11p^{2}+38p-24\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{d\left(p+1\right)\left(-2m^{4}+6m^{3}p-2m^{2}\left(p-1\right)\left(2p-5\right)-12m\left(p^{2}-3p+3\right)-8\left(p^{2}-8p+6\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{d^{2}\left(4mp\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

∎

Remark 7

(Explaining proof techniques)
.

In this subsection, we explain the proof steps more thoroughly than in other places, since most of the techniques repeat themselves throughout the appendices.

C.1.1
Term 1:
\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}

Recalling Remark
4
on our simplified notations, we show that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{\text{i}}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{u}\sim\mathcal{S}^{p-1}}\left(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{u}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{0}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\right)\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{0}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\right)\mathbf{u}\right]\,.

Opening the product above,
by Cor.
12
we are only left with the following terms:

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{0}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{0}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{u}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{u}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\top}\mathbf{u}_{b}\right)^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{u}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\mathbf{u}_{a}\mathbf{u}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\mathbf{u}_{a}\right]\,,

where, like we frequently do throughout the appendix, we decomposed
{\mathbf{u}}
into

{{\mathbf{u}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbf{u}}_{a}\\
{\mathbf{u}}_{b}\end{array}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}

with
{\mathbf{u}}_{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and

{\mathbf{u}}_{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{p-m}
.
This decomposition is often useful, since for two orthogonal unit vectors
{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}}
, it holds that

{0={\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}={\mathbf{u}}_{a}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}_{a}+{\mathbf{u}}_{b}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}_{b}\Longrightarrow{\mathbf{u}}_{a}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}_{a}=-{\mathbf{u}}_{b}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}_{b}}

and

{1=\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}}\right\|^{2}=\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}_{a}}\right\|^{2}+\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}_{b}}\right\|^{2}\Longrightarrow\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}_{a}}\right\|^{2}=1-\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}_{b}}\right\|^{2}}
.

Another “trick” that we use often, is to reparameterize
\mathbf{Q}_{m}{\mathbf{u}}_{a}
(for
\mathbf{Q}_{m}\sim O(m)
) as
\left\|{{\mathbf{u}}_{a}}\right\|{\mathbf{r}}
for
{\mathbf{r}}\sim\mathcal{S}^{m-1}
.
Then, the expectation above becomes,

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{u}_{b}\right\|^{4}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{u}_{a}\right\|^{2}\mathbf{u}_{a}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{m}\mathbf{I}_{m}\right)\mathbf{u}_{a}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{u}_{b}\right\|^{4}\right]+\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{u}_{a}\right\|^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=m+1}^{p}\sum_{j=m+1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}^{2}u_{j}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{i}^{2}u_{j}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(p-m\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{p}^{4}\right]+\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{p-1}^{2}u_{p}^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{m}\left(m\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{4}\right]+m\left(m-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\left(p-m\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{4}\right]+\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{4}\right]+\left(m-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}\right]\,,

where in the last step we used the fact that different entries of
{\mathbf{u}}
are identically distributed (see also Prop.
9
).
Using simple algebraic steps and employing the notations presented in
Appendix
D
for monomials of entries of random orthogonal matrices,
we have

\displaystyle=\left(p-m+1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{4}\right]+\left(m^{2}-2mp+2m+p^{2}-p-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(p-m+1\right)\left\langle\begin{array}[]{c}4\\
\overrightarrow{0}\end{array}\right\rangle+\left(m^{2}-2mp+2m+p^{2}-p-1\right)\left\langle\begin{array}[]{c}2\\
2\\
\overrightarrow{0}\end{array}\right\rangle\,.

Finally, we plug in the expectations (computed in Appendix
D
), and get

\displaystyle=\frac{3\left(p-m+1\right)}{p\left(p+2\right)}+\frac{m^{2}-2mp+2m+p^{2}-p-1}{p\left(p+2\right)}=\frac{m^{2}-2mp-m+p^{2}+2p+2}{p\left(p+2\right)}\,.

C.1.2
Term 2:
\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\right]

It holds that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\sum_{k=1}^{d}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}{\mathbf{e}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right]=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{3}\right]}_{\text{solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e1Oe1)3}}}+\left(d-1\right)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right)^{2}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\right]}_{\text{solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e2Oe1)2_e2Oe_2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(p-m\right)\left(m^{2}-2mp-3m+p^{2}+6p+14\right)}{p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}+\left(d-1\right)\frac{\left(p-m\right)\left(-m^{2}+2mp+3m-6\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(p-m\right)\left(\left(p-1\right)\left(m^{2}-2mp-3m+p^{2}+6p+14\right)+\left(d-1\right)\left(-m^{2}+2mp+3m-6\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(p-m\right)\left(d\left(-m^{2}+2mp+3m-6\right)+m^{2}p-2mp^{2}-3mp+p^{3}+5p^{2}+8p-8\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}

C.1.3
Term 3:
\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{i}\right)^{2}

It holds that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{i}\right)^{2}\!\!=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}\!\!=\mathbb{E}\!\left(\!\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\!\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\!\right)^{\!2}\!\!=\!\mathbb{E}\!\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\!\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\!\right)^{2}\right)^{\!2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}\left(\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}=\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{4}}_{k=\ell}+\underbrace{\sum_{k\neq\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}\left(\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}}_{k\neq\ell}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{4}}_{k=1\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e1Oe1)4}}}+\underbrace{\left(d-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{4}}_{k\geq 2\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e1Oe2)4}}}}_{k=\ell}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\underbrace{\underbrace{2\left(d-1\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}\left(\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}}_{\ell\neq k=1\,\vee\,k\neq\ell=1\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:e2Oe2*e2Oe1}}}+\underbrace{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}\left(\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}}_{k,\ell\geq 2,\,k\neq\ell\text{ solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e2Oe3*e2Oe1)^2}}}}_{k\neq\ell}

We note in passing that since
d\leq p
,
then when
p=2\Rightarrow d\leq 2
, the rightmost term is necessarily zero.
Therefore, we can use Prop.
19
freely
\forall p\geq 2
, even though it requires that
p\geq 3
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{i}\right)^{2}\!

\displaystyle=\tfrac{3\left(m+4\right)\left(m+6\right)+\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m+2\right)\left(m^{2}-2mp-4m+p^{2}+10p+36\right)}{p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{3\left(d-1\right)\left(m^{4}-2m^{3}\left(2p+3\right)+m^{2}\left(4p^{2}+4p-1\right)+2m\left(6p^{2}+8p+3\right)+8\left(p^{2}-2p-3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt2\left(d-1\right)\tfrac{\left(m+4\right)\left(2mp+4p+m-m^{2}-6\right)-\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m+2\right)\left(m\left(m-2p-5\right)+10\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)\left(d-2\right)\left(4mp\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{3\left(m+4\right)\left(m+6\right)+\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m+2\right)\left(m^{2}-2mp-4m+p^{2}+10p+36\right)}{p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)\left(-72+m^{4}\left(-1-2p\right)-72p-20p^{2}-20p^{3}+m^{3}\left(6+16p+8p^{2}\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)\left(m^{2}\left(-47-70p-34p^{2}-10p^{3}\right)+m\left(42+136p+114p^{2}+22p^{3}+4p^{4}\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-1\right)d\left(4mp\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

C.2
Deriving
\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

Lemma 8
.

Let
p\geq 4,m\in\{2,\dots,p\},d\in\left[{p}\right]
,
and let
\mathbf{O}
be a random transformation sampled as described in Eq. (
2.1
).
Then,
\forall i,j\in\left[{d}\right]
such that
i\neq j
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\left(-4p^{3}-6p^{2}+12p\right)m^{4}+m^{3}\left(10p^{4}+14p^{3}+20p^{2}+48p\right)+m^{2}\left(-7p^{5}-4p^{4}-109p^{3}-134p^{2}-120p-144\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{m\left(2p^{6}+3p^{5}+84p^{4}-45p^{3}+336p^{2}+636p+144\right)+\left(-18p^{5}+24p^{4}-182p^{3}-104p^{2}-168p-288\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{3m^{4}\left(-4+4p+3p^{2}\right)-4m^{3}\left(p+2\right)\left(7p^{2}-2p+6\right)+m^{2}\left(26p^{4}+44p^{3}+163p^{2}+256p+36\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{-2m\left(3p^{5}+102p^{3}+142p^{2}+154p+132\right)-2p\left(p+1\right)\left(p^{3}-24p^{2}+26p-204\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd^{2}\cdot\tfrac{m^{4}\left(-5p-6\right)+m^{3}\left(18p^{2}+34p-12\right)+m^{2}\left(-20p^{3}-46p^{2}-39p-42\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd^{2}\cdot\tfrac{m\left(6p^{4}+10p^{3}+96p^{2}+154p+60\right)+\left(2p^{4}-30p^{3}-32p^{2}-144p-144\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}\,.

Proof.

We decompose the expectation as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\left(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{O}\right){\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}-2\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathbf{e}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\right)\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}\,,

and derive each of its three terms separately in the following subsections.
The final result in the lemma is obtained by summing these three terms.
∎

C.2.1
Term 1:
\mathbb{E}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\right)^{2}

It holds that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{u}^{\top}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]+\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\mathbf{v}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{u}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\mathbf{v}_{a}+\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{b}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{u}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\mathbf{v}_{a}\right)^{2}}_{\text{solved in Eq.~(\ref {eq:(uaQmva)^2})}}+\underbrace{2\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{u}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\mathbf{v}_{a}\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{b}\right)}_{=0\text{, by Cor.~\ref {cor:odd-Q_m}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{b}\right)^{2}}_{\text{solved in Eq.~(\ref {eq:(uava)2})}}

\displaystyle=\frac{mp+m-2}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)}+\frac{m\left(p-m\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)}=\frac{mp+m-2+m\left(p-m\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2mp+m-m^{2}-2}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)}

C.2.2
Term 2:
2\left(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{i}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)

It holds that,

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{i}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)\right]=2\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right]=2\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\left(\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right]}_{\text{solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e2Oe1)(e1Oe1)(e1Oe2)}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left(\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\right)^{2}\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right]}_{\text{solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e2Oe1)2_e2Oe_2}}}+\left(d-2\right)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right]}_{\text{solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e2Oe1)(e3Oe1)(e3Oe2)}}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\left(p-m\right)\left(\tfrac{-m^{2}-m+\left(m+1\right)p-2}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}+\tfrac{-m^{2}+2mp+3m-6}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}+\tfrac{\left(d-2\right)\left(2m^{2}-3mp-2m-p+8\right)}{\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=2\left(p-m\right)\left(\frac{-2m^{2}+2m+\left(3m+1\right)p-8}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}+\frac{\left(d-2\right)\left(2m^{2}-3mp-2m-p+8\right)}{\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left(p-m\right)\left(d\left(2m^{2}-3mp-2m-p+8\right)+p\left(-2m^{2}+3mp+2m+p-8\right)\right)}{\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)}

C.2.3
Term 3:
\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)^{2}

It holds that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{d}\!\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{\ell}\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)=\sum_{k,\ell=1}^{d}\!\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)^{2}}_{k=\ell}+\underbrace{\sum_{k\neq\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)}_{k\neq\ell}

We now show that,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)^{2}=\underbrace{2\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)^{2}}_{k=1,2\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:e2Oe2*e2Oe1}}}+\underbrace{\left(d-2\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)^{2}}_{k\geq 3\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e2Oe3*e2Oe1)^2}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{2\left(\left(m+4\right)\left(2mp+4p+m-m^{2}-6\right)-\left(p-m\right)\left(p-m+2\right)\left(m\left(m-2p-5\right)+10\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{\left(d-2\right)\left(4mp\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-2\left(m-p-1\right)\left(m-p\right)\left(m\left(p+2\right)\left(m-2p-5\right)+2\left(5p+6\right)\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{4m\left(-m^{2}+m+4\right)p+4\left(m+1\right)\left(m+2\right)p^{2}+\left(m-6\right)\left(m-1\right)m\left(m+1\right)-8\left(2p+3\right)}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

Moreover, we have that,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq\ell=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{2\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)}_{k=1,\ell=2\,\vee\,k=2,\ell=1\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e1Oe1)(e1Oe2)(e2Oe1)(e2Oe2)}}}+\underbrace{4\left(d-2\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)}_{k\leq 2,\ell\geq 3\,\vee\,k\geq 3,\ell\leq 2\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e1Oe1)(e1Oe2)(e3Oe1)(e3Oe2)}.}}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\underbrace{\left(d-2\right)\left(d-3\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)\left(\mathbf{e}_{4}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{1}\mathbf{e}_{4}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{2}\right)}_{k\neq\ell\geq 3\text{, solved in Prop.~\ref {prop:(e3Oe4)(e3Oe1)(e2Oe4)(e2Oe1)}}}

By summing all of the above and by some tedious algebraic steps,
we finally get that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{+}\bm{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{e}_{j}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{2p\left(m^{4}\left(6-3p-2p^{2}\right)+m^{3}\left(-12-20p+15p^{2}+7p^{3}\right)+m^{2}\left(18+13p+5p^{2}-21p^{3}-8p^{4}\right)\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\tfrac{2p\left(m\left(3p^{5}+8p^{4}+21p^{3}+28p^{2}-14p-12\right)+p\left(12-4p-29p^{2}-12p^{3}+p^{4}\right)\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{3m^{4}\left(-4+4p+3p^{2}\right)-4m^{3}\left(-6-13p+16p^{2}+8p^{3}\right)+m^{2}\left(-36+16p+29p^{2}+88p^{3}+36p^{4}\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd\cdot\tfrac{-2m\left(6p^{5}+13p^{4}+69p^{3}+105p^{2}+16p-12\right)+\left(-4p^{5}+54p^{4}+90p^{3}+152p^{2}+120p\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd^{2}\cdot\tfrac{m^{4}\left(-5p-6\right)+m^{3}\left(18p^{2}+34p-12\right)+m^{2}\left(-20p^{3}-46p^{2}-39p-42\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}+

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002ptd^{2}\cdot\tfrac{m\left(6p^{4}+10p^{3}+96p^{2}+154p+60\right)+\left(2p^{4}-30p^{3}-32p^{2}-144p-144\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}\,.

.

.

Empirically illustrating the worst-case forgetting under different overparameterization levels.

\mathbf{X}
.

.

Appendix D
Monomials of Entries of Random Orthogonal Matrices

Throughout the supplementary materials, we often wish to compute the expectation of an arbitrary monomial of the entries
of a random orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{Q}\sim O(p)
sampled uniformly from the orthogonal group,

e.g.,
\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}^{2}q_{1,2}^{4}q_{2,2}^{6}]
.
Following
Gorin 2002
, we define a “power matrix”
{\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{p\times R}}

(for some
R\leq p
)
that maps into a monomial

\prod_{i,j=1}^{p,R}q_{i,j}^{m_{i,j}}

constructed from the entries
of the first
R
columns of
the random
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
.
We denote the expected value of this monomial by

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}\left[{\prod}_{i,j=1}^{p,R}q_{i,j}^{m_{i,j}}\right]\triangleq\left\langle\mathbf{M}\right\rangle\,,\quad\text{for example, }\quad\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}^{2}q_{2,1}^{4}q_{2,2}^{6}]\triangleq\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}2&0\\
4&6\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\,.

We employ the notation
\overrightarrow{0}
to complement
\mathbf{M}
to have
p
rows.
For instance, in the example above,
\overrightarrow{0}
is a vector with
p\!-\!2
zero entries.

The following are helpful properties of the integral (expectation) over the orthogonal group, as mentioned in
Gorin 2002
.

Property 9

(Invariance of the integral over the orthogonal group)
.

Let
\mathbf{Q}\sim O(p)
and
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{p\times R}
.

1.

Invariance w.r.t. transpose.

Since
\mathbf{Q}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\top}
are identically distributed, it holds that
{\left\langle\mathbf{M}\right\rangle=\langle\mathbf{M}^{\top}\rangle}
. For example,

\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}2&0\\
4&2\\
4&6\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}^{2}q_{2,1}^{4}q_{2,2}^{2}q_{3,1}^{4}q_{3,2}^{6}]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}^{2}q_{1,2}^{4}q_{1,3}^{4}q_{2,2}^{2}q_{2,3}^{6}]=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}2&4&4\\
0&2&6\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\,.

2.

Invariance w.r.t. row and column permutations.

Since different rows/columns of
\mathbf{Q}
are identically distributed, the integral over the orthogonal group
O(p)
is invariant under permutations of columns or rows of the power matrix
\mathbf{M}
(see also
Ullah 1964
).
For example,

\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}2&0\\
4&6\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}^{2}q_{2,1}^{4}q_{2,2}^{6}]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}^{6}q_{1,2}^{4}q_{2,2}^{2}]=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}6&4\\
0&2\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\,.

The following is a known property of odd moments in integrals (expectations) over the orthogonal group
(see
Brody et al. 1981
;
Gorin 2002
).

Property 10
.

If the sum over
any
row or column of the power matrix
\mathbf{M}
is odd, the integral vanishes, i.e.,
\langle\mathbf{M}\rangle=0
.
For example,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}}[q_{1,1}q_{2,1}^{4}q_{2,2}^{6}]=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
4&6\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=0
.

(In contrast, generally, it holds that

\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\neq 0
).

Corollary 11
.

Let
{\mathbf{q}}_{i}
be the
i
th
column (or row) of
\mathbf{Q}
.
Let
\mathbf{A}^{(1)},\dots,\mathbf{A}^{(N)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
be
N
matrices independent on
\mathbf{Q}
and let
i_{1},\dots,i_{2N}\in\left[{p}\right]
be
2N
indices.
Then, if there exists an index
i\in\left[{p}\right]
that appears an
odd
number of times in
i_{1},\dots,i_{2N}
, the following expectation vanishes:

\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{A}^{(i_{1})}{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{N+1}}\cdot{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{2}}\mathbf{A}^{(i_{2})}{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{N+2}}\cdots{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{N}}\mathbf{A}^{(i_{N})}{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{2N}}]=0\,.

For example, the above dictates that,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{Q}_{m}}\bigg[{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\bigg]=0\,,

since
{\mathbf{q}}_{1}
appears
3
times (also because
{\mathbf{q}}_{3}
appears once).

Proof.

The expectation can be rewritten as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{A}^{(i_{1})}{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{N+1}}\cdots{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{N}}\mathbf{A}^{(i_{N})}{\mathbf{q}}_{i_{2N}}]=\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{N}=1}^{p}\sum_{k_{1},\dots,k_{N}=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left[{\prod}_{\ell=1}^{N}q_{j_{\ell},i_{\ell}}a^{(i_{\ell})}_{j_{\ell},k_{\ell}}q_{k_{\ell},i_{N+{\ell}}}\right]\,.

Let
t\in\left[{p}\right]
be an index that appears an odd number of times in
i_{1},\dots,i_{2N}
.
The
t
th
column (or row) appears the same number of times in each of the (summand) expectations.
Thus, the sum of the
t
th
column (or row) corresponding to
{\mathbf{q}}_{t}
in the power matrix
\mathbf{M}
corresponding to that expectation will be an odd number.
Thus, by Prop.
10
, the expectation vanishes.
∎

Corollary 12
.

Let
{\mathbf{v}}_{1},\dots,{\mathbf{v}}_{2N}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}

and
c\in\mathbb{R}

be random variables independent of
\mathbf{Q}_{m}\sim O(m)
.
Then, if
N
is an odd number, the following expectation vanishes:

\mathbb{E}[c\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\top}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathbf{v}}_{N+1}\cdot{\mathbf{v}}_{2}^{\top}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathbf{v}}_{N+2}\cdots{\mathbf{v}}_{N}^{\top}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathbf{v}}_{2N}]=0\,.

For example, the above dictates that the following expectation vanishes,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{Q}_{m}}\bigg[{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\cdot{\mathbf{e}}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\bigg]=0\,,

since here
N=3
.

In contrast, the above does
not
imply that the following expectation vanishes,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{Q}_{m}}\bigg[{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}\cdot\underbrace{{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{I}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{1}}_{\text{here, this is considered as $c$}}\cdot\,{\mathbf{e}}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}_{p}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{p-m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}_{p}^{\top}{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\bigg]\,,

since here
N=2
.

Proof.

The expectations become,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[c\cdot{\prod}_{\ell=1}^{N}{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell}^{\top}\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{Q}_{m}\\
&\mathbf{0}_{\ell}\end{smallmatrix}\right]{\mathbf{v}}_{N+\ell}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[c\cdot{\prod}_{\ell=1}^{N}\bigg({{\sum}_{i,j=1}^{m}({\mathbf{v}}_{\ell})_{i}\underbrace{(\mathbf{Q}_{m})_{i,j}}_{\triangleq q_{i,j}}({\mathbf{v}}_{N+\ell})_{j}}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[c\cdot{\prod}_{\ell=1}^{N}\left({{\sum}_{i,j=1}^{m}q_{i,j}({\mathbf{v}}_{\ell})_{i}({\mathbf{v}}_{N+\ell})_{j}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{N}=1}^{m}\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{N}=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[c\cdot{\prod}_{\ell=1}^{N}q_{i_{\ell},j_{\ell}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\ell})_{i_{\ell}}({\mathbf{v}}_{N+\ell})_{j_{\ell}}\right]\,.

We notice that in each of the (summand) expectations the entries of
\mathbf{Q}_{m}
appear exactly
N
times. However, since
N
is odd, at least one row or column of the power matrix corresponding to the monomial in the expectation must have an odd sum.
Then, by Prop.
10
, all these expectations vanish.
∎

The main result we need from
Gorin 2002
is their Eq. (23), providing a recursive formula to compute
\langle\mathbf{M}\rangle
for any power matrix
\mathbf{M}
.
We bring this formula here for the sake of completeness.

Lemma 13

(Recursive formula for computing expectations of monomials over the orthogonal group)
.

Define the Pochhammer symbol
(z)_{n}=\frac{\Gamma(z+n)}{\Gamma(z)}
.
Denote

\binom{\overrightarrow{n}}{\overrightarrow{k}}=\prod_{i=1}^{p}\binom{n_{i}}{k_{i}}
.
Denote
(\overrightarrow{n}|K)=\prod_{i=1}^{p}\left(n_{i}\,\mid\,K_{i,1},\dots,K_{i,R-1}\right)
.

Then, the one-vector integral is given by

\langle\overrightarrow{m}\rangle=\left({\frac{p}{2}}\right)^{-1}_{\overline{m}/2}\prod_{i=1}^{p}\left({\frac{1}{2}}\right)_{m_{i}/2}.

Moreover, the
R
-vector integral (corresponding to a matrix
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{p\times R}
, is given by

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{M}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left({\tfrac{p-R+1}{2}}\right)^{-1}_{{\overline{m}_{R}}/{2}}\cdot

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{\overrightarrow{\kappa}}\!\left({\!\binom{\overline{m}_{R}}{\overrightarrow{\kappa}}\left({-1}\right)^{\left({\overline{m}_{R}-\overline{\kappa}}\right)/2}\cdot{\prod_{i=1}^{p}\!\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)_{{\kappa_{i}}/{2}}}\cdot\sum_{K}(\overrightarrow{m}_{R}\!-\!\overrightarrow{\kappa}|K)\cdot{\prod_{j=1}^{R-1}\!\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)_{{\overline{k}_{j}}/{2}}}\cdot\langle\mathbf{M}^{(R-1)}\!+\!K\rangle\!}\right)

where the first sum runs over all
\overrightarrow{\kappa}
with all even entries (less or equal to the corresponding entries of the last column
\overrightarrow{m}_{R}
).
The second sum runs over all
K\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{p,R-1}
for which all sums of columns are even,
i.e.,
\overline{k}_{j}\triangleq\sum_{i=1}^{p}k_{i,j}
is even
\forall j\in\left[{R-1}\right]
.
Finally,

\mathbf{M}^{(R-1)}
stands for the first
R-1
columns of
\mathbf{M}
,
and
\overline{m}_{R}=\sum_{i=1}^{p}m_{i,R}

and
\overline{\kappa}=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\kappa_{i}
.

In the pages to come, we present many calculations of expectations of different monomials that we use throughout the supplementary materials.
We include these calculations since the recursive formula of Lemma
13
is somewhat complicated to apply, and we wish our derivations to be reproducible and easily followed.

D.1
Monomials of One Orthogonal Vector

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{1}^{4}]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}4\\
\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=\left({\tfrac{p}{2}}\right)^{-1}_{2}\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)_{2}=\left(\tfrac{p}{2}\left(\tfrac{p+2}{2}\right)\right)^{-1}\tfrac{3}{4}=\tfrac{3}{p(p+2)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{1}^{6}]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}6\\
\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=\left({\tfrac{p}{2}}\right)^{-1}_{3}\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)_{3}=\tfrac{15}{p(p+2)(p+4)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}2\\
2\\
\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=\left({\tfrac{p}{2}}\right)^{-1}_{2}\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)_{1}\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)_{1}=\left(\tfrac{p}{2}\left(\tfrac{p+2}{2}\right)\right)^{-1}\tfrac{1}{4}=\tfrac{1}{p(p+2)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{1}^{4}u_{2}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}4\\
2\\

\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right)

\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right)

\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle-3\left\langle\begin{smallmatrix}4&2&2\\
\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{p-3}{\left(p-3\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}=\tfrac{1}{p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

\overrightarrow{0}&\overrightarrow{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right\rangle=\tfrac{p+5}{\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\left(p-2\right)\left(p+3\right)\left(p^{2}+6p+1\right)}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{p^{2}+5p+18}{\left(p-3\right)\left(p-2\right)\left(p-1\right)p\left(p+1\right)\left(p+2\right)\left(p+4\right)\left(p+6\right)}
